# ConvexPress production-readiness implementation ledger

Active goal: complete the revalidated Astra audit and Claude handoff, then prove production readiness for isolated client websites.

Worktree: `/Users/worsin/.codex/worktrees/convexpress-hardening`; branch `codex/convexpress-hardening`. Application source and Claude handoffs were integrated into main at `ee7087f4` on September20. Later verified fixes are recorded below; original live processes are preserved. The initial audit started at `3816d7af`. Historical process notes below are checkpoints, not current process inventory; inspect actual process paths before acceptance.

No item is complete until implementation, regression verification and applicable runtime acceptance are recorded. Scope comes from `astra-audit.md`, Claude's handoff and the user's agency/client/database requirements. User authorized all implementation; provider accounts, live deployment and client acceptance must use verified targets and credentials, never guesses. Do not send external messages.

## Integration boundaries and decisions

- Content agent owns post/page READ queries, feeds/search and public-access helpers. Root owns post/page WRITE scheduling and revisions. Coordinate search refresh via emitted post/page update events.
- Commerce agent owns commerce/subscriptions/returns, commerce schemas and refund webhook cases; root owns schema barrel only.
- Auth agent owns control-plane hierarchy/RBAC/session and desktop sender/auth storage. Root waits before changing those files for provisioning work.
- Templates already shipped 86 surfaces in Core/Journal/Depot; preserve view models while repairing access DTOs.
- Legacy removals require migration first. Existing data and original source checkout remain intact until safe integration.
- Single browser-driving owner (root); no agent may kill shared ports or launch competing browser acceptance.
- Dependencies installed from frozen lockfiles. Extension schema index generated in this worktree; new Aster House production and staging cloud backends deployed; fleet control plane refresh pending.
- Existing repair design was explicitly approved by user; no redundant design approval gate. Larger missing features receive concrete design notes and implementation under that authorization.

## Native voting and the real concurrency failure

Electron created and saved the dedicated A slower morning page, reopened its exact question/four options, and acknowledged a Website preview with all response controls disabled. Native publication then enabled the real public host. Clicking the visible coast choice and submitting produced one persisted vote, a server-derived100% result and focused confirmation. The initial automation tried `.check()` on the visually hidden radio and was intercepted; the visible-label interaction succeeded without force or DOM event injection.

The first real concurrent HTTP test failed with OptimisticConcurrencyControlFailure on form_poll_tallies after Convex exhausted its transaction retries. Its failed receipt is retained, and already accepted votes were not erased. Eight requests sharing one token still produced exactly one recorded choice; one of eight independent requests failed, leaving9 total votes including the original browser vote. This was a real contention failure, not a successful acceptance.

Added the shared bounded retryPollWrite helper: retry only recognized OCC commit failures, keep the exact request identity, use exponential delay with jitter, stop after5attempts, and stop client retry after session/authority change. Unknown, network, CAPTCHA, rate and authorization errors are not automatically retried. For CAPTCHA actions, retry only the final mutation; do not reuse the external challenge or extend the original verification timestamp. This adds burst resilience, not a claim of unlimited shared-counter throughput. Convex documents this shared-document conflict behavior at https://docs.convex.dev/error .

Corrected live HTTP acceptance using the production helper passed:8concurrent same-identity requests→1vote,8independent identities→8votes, final18. The open public browser updated reactively while retaining coast. Native remove/save removed the public form; restoring Revision3 as currentRevision5 returned its exact question/choice identities, all18vote rows, the byte-identical tally and the guest's original selected answer. The removed/restored state receipts compare exactly. Emails0, prior queue2, Contact revision5/1block and field guide revision20/10blocks unchanged.

Retry/helper and registered Poll tests15/135 pass; mounted renderer harness, Website types,38API fixtures and final generated/whitespace checks pass. Final workerd hosting again passes all four packs. Native publish artifactbf93ae515e2ce7cb463fd2612084a6178556bd30cdac6e5163b71390d12d4c65 is verified in public headers. Reloading it preserves18responses and the disabled recorded choice. Inspected native saved preview, public desktop and278px-wide mobile form; no horizontal overflow. Staging capture r2 stopped before deploy because fresh codegen added the expected pollRetry module to api.d.ts. The exact fresh binding was inspected/copied and recaptured as r3; its final deploy is tracked by its separate receipt.

## Product creation, media legacy references and base pricing — September 6

Fixed a native product-creation failure caused by the generated slug `pricingwindowacceptance`. Convex normalizes this ordinary text as a legacy media ID (`tx7gxswt6rhrg16zdcpge7h7xxkajpww`), even when that media does not exist. The shared extractor now records whether a reference is explicit/canonical or an ambiguous legacy candidate. Missing ambiguous prose is ignored; typed and canonical missing attachments remain rejected. Existing legacy aliases remain indexed and are discovered during deletion checks, including nested serialized JSON. Both reads obey bounded metadata and request budgets. No slug workaround or weakened genuine attachment policy was used.

A second native save exposed explicit featured-image clearing: the editor correctly sends null, but the product update handler stored null in an optional ID field. The handler now translates explicit null to field removal while preserving omission and valid selected IDs. Regression coverage exercises all three cases.

The deployment writer gate found three raw contact-generated field definition writes. These now use the shared guarded insert/patch helpers, preserving request accounting and same-transaction index maintenance. The regenerated writer signature intentionally changes the media index generation. The native authorized maintenance runner rebuilt all 26 owner tables automatically: 123 documents, 37 pages, Ready, no error. The staging epoch remains unchanged.

Backend pricing now shares zero-sale, date-boundary and invalid-sale handling across catalog/cart/variant helpers. Unpublished default variants no longer set the public product summary price. This covers base pricing; contextual pricing, full public product DTO safety, scalability and frontend variant pricing still require work described in next-commerce-work.md.

## Featured Products source reader and renderer — September 6

Implemented core/featured-products as a dynamic block using the new strict commerce.featuredProducts portable contract. Saved manual product IDs retain their original order after deduplication; an explicitly empty/missing/denied selection never falls back to latest products. Default discovery selects the latest added products through by_status_created, with bounded sequential reads and explicit budget refusal rather than a silently truncated catalog. This is the capped Featured Products block source, not a replacement for the global paginated storefront catalog.

Variable card selection uses the new by_product_status_default index and existing by_product_status index: at most four one-row reads, deterministic earliest public default/fallback, and legacy absent-status compatibility. A test with 300 hidden defaults proves those rows do not need scanning. Product and route membership rules are both enforced with the request ledger; revocation removes cards. Full fetched product/variant/media sources are measured before dependent reads, including fields omitted from the public DTO. The result contains only title/link/excerpt/image/date/optional price inputs; no ownership, import metadata, license configuration or inventory audit data. Bundle-owned and future/draft products are omitted. The reader is a service dependency supplied from the current-document adapter, never a caller-selected function.

Added a responsive four-card renderer using existing pack typography/primitives and the shared sale timer. It renders real source prices, optional comparison/sale labels, escaped titles, image/typographic missing-image states, and product links. It does not advertise inventory availability or perform direct Add to cart. New isolated BlockDemo adapter uses existing synthetic product images and never enters production readers. Browser screenshots cover Core, Journal, Depot and Aster at 1440/390px; all four images load, section overflow checks pass, keyboard focus is visible and reduced-motion removes transitions. First screenshot was captured before lazy images finished; final captures explicitly decode images. A screenshot taken during CSS HMR showed the prior three-column layout; the subsequent computed style and full four-pack captures show the final four/two/one-column grid.

Validation: 441 backend/canonical/commerce tests with 5952 assertions; 115 renderer tests with 3066 assertions, including every canonical example in every discovered pack. Backend and Website types pass. Fresh source Convex codegen/typecheck passes. Initial codegen exposed an unused TS2589 suppression on commerceBundles/mutations.ts productId; removed only that stale comment, then fresh codegen passed. The renderer runner requires Bun; the first invocation used Node and failed before running tests, then the proper Bun run exposed the new demo plugin/provider requirement; wired the real synthetic product adapter and commerce policy, followed by the successful complete run. The legacy compatibility source-hash manifest was regenerated after block spec changes; final generated check passes. API contracts: 2076 functions,2466 terminal DTOs,427 existing unknown boundaries,19 compiler fixtures each consumer.

NOT activated or deployed yet. The production document service still intentionally disables unsupported product reference fields, and the native resource picker has no product option path. Complete its typed authorized pagination endpoint and UI integration, then enable the exact allowlisted resolver and perform registered/native save/publish/revision/full-restore acceptance. Current live staging backend remains storefront-pricing-final-20260906 and Website remains f5d4af31fb23. No new backend checkpoint was captured or deployed in this step. Accepted renderer count stays98/136 despite99 source implementations. MagicTables core/featured-products received a Notes-only checkpoint with exact dry run/readback; all other fields, including Tests/Screenshots, remain unchanged.

Additional confirmed commerce prerequisite: cart.ts assertSufficientStock exits early when parent tracking is off, ignores explicit variant manageStock=no, and does not inherit parent backorder policy for manageStock=parent. Checkout/inventory and Website have separate conflicting rules. This remains a required shared-policy repair, alongside global catalog scaling and product-collection modes, not something this display reader fixes. Full original audit, all136blocks/fourpacks/SDK, customer/fleet isolation, hosting/recovery and clean-machine packaging goal remains active. Original checkout, production database and prior runtimes preserved; no commits/pushes.

Evidence: output/product-block-reader-20260906/checks.json, outcome.md, source-codegen-final.log, backend-regression-tests.log, render-tests-final.log, screenshots and tracking readback.

Final Featured Products consumer check: full Admin and Website typechecks passed after API contract regeneration.

## Shared stock ownership and reservation lifecycle — September 6 (source, not deployed)

Unified explicit yes/no/parent inventory ownership and legacy variant behavior through a portable policy used by cart, checkout, inventory, order/payment allocation, bundle availability, Website product state and Admin variant drafts. New reservations retain physical parent/variant/location ownership; order allocation records carry orderId and new orders carry inventoryPolicyVersion=1 so restoration can follow the recorded owner without inventing stock for untracked orders. Warehouse and draft-product inventory detail queries now require manage_options. These changes remain incomplete pending the conflict/allocation work below.

This continuation reproduced and fixed seven additional defects: checkout cancellation released only the first hold and depended on current/deleted variant selection; reservation-specific cancellation ignored a conflicting checkout ID; expiry scanned the reservation table and did not continue its batch; untracked variant adjustments read parent quantity; fulfillment checked sibling variants independently despite shared stock; parent adjustment audit records retained the selected variant and parent inventory-list entries could not be adjusted; warehouse edits of parent-mode variants created separate variant levels. Release now clears all active checkout holds, remains idempotent after target deletion, and preserves physical owner/location audit data. Expiry uses by_status_expiry in 100-row batches with scheduled continuation. Fulfillment shares planned quantities across each physical owner. Explicit runtime return validators were added to reservation/release/commit/adjustment/warehouse/fulfillment endpoints changed in this work.

Validation: 313 commerce/bundle/Admin helper tests pass with 768 assertions across 22 files. That includes 21 in-memory registered-handler stock cases and two schema-backed convex-test cases covering indexed reservations/releases and refusal before partial release. These do not prove live-cloud OCC/concurrency. Seven new red regressions and their passing reruns are retained. Fresh source Convex codegen/typecheck, full Admin and Website types, and compiler contract fixtures pass. API generator emits 2077 functions, 2469 terminal DTOs and 421 remaining unknown boundaries. Writer coverage verifies 1300 classified writes, 26 owner tables, 17 typed media-reference tables and no bypasses. First frontend typecheck commands had incorrect log paths; corrected absolute paths completed successfully.

MagicTables core/featured-products received one Notes-only prerequisite update after exact dry run. Readback confirms the Notes match and all other cells remain unchanged. Accepted renderers stay 98/136. No new stock checkpoint deployment or Website publish: live backend remains featured-products-native-20260906, media epoch mi_ready_6ea2d8de05174e61835500e69f395847, Website artifact 074e704df6abe7321eb80b22dfefc418c630e36ad4a0b36a1ffca23081917e7e. Production, original checkout and existing native/browser runtimes preserved; no commits/pushes.

Required next: reject or explicitly reconcile expired/changed-stock reservations at commitment without losing captured payment state; unify warehouse/global availability and allocation; finish bundle stock-cap and manual-order lifecycle; impose request-wide read budgets and remove remaining whole-table inventory reads; enforce safe signed quantities across admin stock writes; then immutable checkpoint/preflight/deploy and native/public/cloud acceptance. Historical orders without allocation receipts still require a deliberate compatibility/review path. The full original audit/handoff, 136-block/four-pack/SDK/BlockDemo, customer/fleet isolation, hosting/recovery and clean-machine packaging goal remains active. Evidence and exact continuation: output/stock-policy-20260906/checks-release-owner.json and next-work.md.


## Reservation commitment and captured-payment conflicts — September 6 (source, not deployed)

Added a typed shared reservation preflight for offline checkout and the internal inventory commit. It reads/validates the entire allocation before any stock write, combines quantities belonging to the same physical owner, checks current safety stock, validates safe whole quantities, and rejects expired holds, mismatched order/checkout identities, released orders, and missing/inactive/ambiguous warehouse targets. Holds now snapshot backorder permission; new checkout orders snapshot expected reservation count and inventoryPolicyVersion=1. Missing/expired holds on these orders cannot silently fall back to allocation under today's ownership settings. Commit replay preserves the original timestamp and stock.

Payment confirmation now treats recognized reserved-inventory domain conflicts as fulfillment review while retaining the succeeded payment. It skips the legacy fallback after either a successful reserved commit or a recorded conflict. Five new red regression cases reproduced expired holds, stock depletion, removed holds, partial multi-item commitment and lost backorder permission; all now pass. The existing fallback reservation query was changed to the bounded indexed reader. This does not finish legacy/direct-order allocation, bundle stock caps or all payment-reconciliation failure modes.

Validation: 321 commerce/bundle/Admin helper tests pass with 810 assertions across 22 files. Includes five schema-backed cases, using actual nested inventory/payment mutations and actual purchases.syncCommerceOrder: the conflict case retains succeeded transaction, unchanged stock, order/purchase needs_review and single payment/review history after replay; the successful case deducts original stock once and synchronizes a paid purchase. These are local Convex test-adapter checks, not live-cloud/provider acceptance. Fresh source codegen/backend types, full Admin and Website types, and 19 compiler contract fixtures per consumer pass. Generated contracts: 2077 functions, 2469 terminal DTOs, 421 remaining unknown boundaries. Media writer check: 1301 classified writes, 26 owner tables, 17 typed media-reference tables, no bypasses. git diff --check passes.

MagicTables core/featured-products received a Notes-only checkpoint at the 320-test stage, verified by exact dry run and readback with every other field unchanged; one final happy-payment schema test raised the suite to 321. No block completion flags changed; accepted renderers remain 98/136. No stock deployment or Website publish. Live staging/backend/epoch/artifact remain the Featured Products native checkpoint recorded above. No commits/pushes or original-checkout/production changes.

Next: warehouse/global stock availability aligned with shipping origin/rate provenance, bundle caps/component allocation, legacy/direct order compatibility, bounded request-wide inventory reads and safe signed admin quantity writes, then immutable staging checkpoint/native/public/cloud acceptance. Existing legacy fallback still resolves variants before its catch and skips missing products; do not claim all captured-payment conflict paths are fixed. Readonly inspection also finds checkout stock location selection independent of quote provenance and no shipFromLocationId assignment in checkout.ts; inspect this before adding warehouse splitting. The entire original production-readiness goal remains active. Evidence: output/stock-policy-20260906/checks-commit-conflicts.json, commit-conflicts-before.log, commit-conflicts-after.log, commit-schema-transactions.log, commerce-suite-commit-payment-final.log and next-work.md.


## Shipping quote provenance and warehouse alignment — September 6 (source, not deployed)

Reproduced five defects: quote persistence discarded supplied origin/package/account data; invalid prices could replace valid quotes; checkout reserved a different warehouse than the quoted origin; insufficient origin stock borrowed a second warehouse; and changed warehouse addresses did not invalidate quotes. Added strict quote/origin/package contracts and validated complete replacement input before deleting previous quotes, with explicit 500-quote read/write limits and duplicate-key rejection. Preserved exact supplied provenance. An explicit RegisteredMutation signature resolves the existing shipping/internals generic-depth errors without new suppressions; initial failing compiler log retained.

All five V2 carrier rate adapters now snapshot their actual settings origin and supplied package inputs. Manual pipeline quotes carry the warehouse origin resolved for the pipeline. Checkout stores the quoted location on the order, checks current warehouse identity/activity/address, and limits physical inventory allocation to that location. Location reads are bounded, duplicate levels refused, and unavailable configured warehouse stock cannot silently use unrelated aggregate inventory. Public quote query/action return display fields without warehouse addresses, account IDs, package data or raw carrier responses. Public rate actions also project zone id/name and omit internal diagnostic stages; server diagnostics remain recorded.

## Assistant session ownership, private facets and rate enforcement — September 10

September 10 assistant session and cache hardening: assistant reads/writes now verify both persisted session and cart ownership against the current site-local user. Signed-in sessions bind their owner; another account or an anonymous caller cannot use the token to access their history, memory, feedback or clearing operations. UUID validation prevents user IDs from being interpreted as legacy guest-memory keys. New signed-in memories use the same user subject for model writes, list and deletion; guest memory remains accessible in its original adopted session. Browser tokens rotate on sign-out/account changes, and a backend settlement mutation replaces legacy invalid or foreign-owned tokens before cart/assistant queries subscribe. Unmapped Clerk profiles can use an unowned guest session, but cannot satisfy account ownership; inactive users are refused.

Briefs are session-scoped and old unscoped rows are not returned. Personalized facet chips remain in private briefs; both catalog consumers now use the owning session to retrieve them. Explicitly curated public chips and banned-query overrides remain supported. Thread clearing hides prior turns immediately and schedules250-message deletion batches with a cutoff that preserves later turns. Atomic rolling per-session rate limits count user turns independently of the display-history window and survive clearing. Direct memory and cart-line reads are capped; relation/catalog helper read bounds still need follow-through; oversize legacy memory over40 facts requires clearing, with up to200 facts cleared per subject before explicit maintenance refusal.

## Cart and checkout ownership — September 10

## Contact promotion authority and document-local block IDs — September 11

The preceding user-requested status-report turn was no progress. This continuation completed the contact permission implementation's real registered API acceptance, fixed an additional shared-template identity defect, and deployed both fixes only to the disposable Linux source4860/target4870 pair. Review and canonical writing now share contactWriteRequirement, an indexed bounded lookup by destination post ID plus contact block ID. Review checks form.create for a new backing form and form.update for an existing form, including descendants. Required capabilities are cached within one plan, and backing-form revisions join the reviewed dependency fingerprint. Missing permission produces TARGET_CONTACT_PERMISSION without a receipt; apply replans and rejects revoked permissions or changed backing-form identity before document writes. The lightweight helper preserves the prior registered writer boundary that avoids loading the full canonical writer into export queries.

A second defect treated canonical block IDs as globally unique across an entire promotion selection. The canonical contract explicitly defines uniqueness within each document. Validation now delegates canonical identity checks to that tree contract while keeping the global namespace for legacy block membership policies. Separate template pages can reuse local block IDs; duplicates within a canonical document and collisions across legacy pages remain rejected. A canonical-local ID cannot satisfy a legacy global block policy target.

## Account and native site-switch acceptance — final September 11 checkpoint

Both isolated database deployments succeeded from sealed1244-file canonical-account-teaser-20260911. Final8 backend tests38 assertions include active Clerk-backed customer, banned customer, inactive local user, source-provider mismatch and unregistered identity.176 renderer tests3747 assertions, Website/BlockDemo/backend/Admin/Electron/preload types, Website/Admin builds and isolated desktop-main bundle passed. The later added customer assertions changed only a test file after snapshot sealing; deployed runtime matches tested source.

Native acceptance uncovered two site-switch defects. The renderer previously registered origins and started the site client concurrently, treated persisted origins as current-document CSP authority, and could lose reload intent on effect replay. Main now tracks the actual delivered origin policy by webContents ID; IPC returns reloadRequired from that document policy. Renderer caches the registration promise, waits for current-document readiness before session exchange/client creation, and exposes retry/reopen on failure. It no longer records a sessionStorage reload marker before a successful navigation.23 tests108 assertions cover origins, effect replay, failure retry, document isolation and route transitions. A real isolated two-window Electron harness imported production security IPC/CSP modules: first and repeated registration required reload; a second preexisting window independently required reload; network reads were denied before each document reload and succeeded after it. Test PID59818 closed; temporary profile removed. Initial harness attempts failed due to its module-mode and bundled preload-path setup, were corrected, and are excluded from passing evidence.

The second defect kept the previous database's resource URL when changing sites. Explicit scope changes now navigate to /dashboard before setting the destination scope, honor cancelled editor navigation, and ignore superseded requests. Main Electron verified switching from Promotion Lab target back to Aster staging lands on /dashboard with zero console errors/one existing development warning. It was then returned to the saved Our makers editor. No Aster content mutation, cloud deployment, commit or push occurred. The main process remains the existing PID39198; it has not been restarted into the newly built main-process policy code, so fresh full packaged-app acceptance remains open despite the isolated real Electron policy proof.

## KB search provider acceptance — 2026-09-11

2026-09-11 — External KB search authorization, provider boundaries and confirmed Meilisearch tasks.

Index-writing actions now require manage_options; internal indexing steps recheck that authority. Search checks current Knowledge Base/route access before contacting providers and returns current database metadata. Meilisearch indexes are derived from site/environment/runtime identity. Provider keys use the shared secret envelope; changing embedding providers clears a retained old key in both settings APIs. OpenAI is the supported embedding option; legacy Anthropic configurations fail before any key is sent to OpenAI. Provider HTTP requests reject redirects, use a 15-second request timeout, bound JSON to 2 MiB and omit raw provider errors.

Meilisearch sync now initializes missing status/category filters, confirms the exact provider task and expected index, clears a prior synced flag before indexing, and marks completion only when the article document, namespace and provider configuration still match. A 30-second observation window reports pending without resubmitting. This is not durable background recovery. Failed/canceled tasks and concurrent content/configuration changes never become confirmed syncs in the covered tests.

63 tests / 527 assertions / 6 files pass. Backend fresh deploy typechecks, Admin/Website types, terminal contracts (2116 functions, 2593 DTOs, 404 existing unknown boundaries), media writer coverage and diff check pass. Earlier live completion failed while resolving runtime identity through a nested query; direct identity reading in the completion transaction fixed the live failure. The failed attempts were not counted as accepted.

Source sync2470ms, target3327ms (single acceptance articles, not a throughput claim). Each key was denied access to the other index with HTTP403; the other site's article was absent. ## Durable KB search jobs — 2026-09-11

2026-09-11 — Durable Meilisearch job foundation and live queued indexing.

Replaced synchronous public sync/remove implementations with persistent per-operation jobs. Each job captures the operator principal (no JWT), provider configuration fingerprint, environment index, source fingerprint, provider task acknowledgement and worker lease. The current job for an article blocks conflicting operations until its outcome is known. Completed job IDs/results are retained when later operations start. Scheduled workers and a bounded recovery sweep resume known tasks after a lease expires. Every new provider step rechecks live authority/configuration; a different authorized operator can resume a paused known task. An uncertain write acknowledgement fences subsequent writes rather than being resubmitted. A late acknowledgement can leave quarantine and is retained before the next authority check. Removal now clears the local meilisearchSynced flag. Removed obsolete synchronous prepare/mark functions so normal writes use the job path.

Public syncArticle/removeArticle now return a typed job descriptor. Poll kb/searchJobs:status and use resume for paused jobs; callers must not assume the action return means indexing is complete. Operator-facing UI, automatic indexing triggers/bulk controls and uncertain-acknowledgement reconciliation are not implemented yet. An uncertain job deliberately cannot be resumed as a new write. This is safe fencing, not complete recovery UX.

69 tests /560 assertions /6 files pass. Tests cover known-task resume without another document write, inactive original operator and a fresh authorized operator, lost acknowledgement fencing, conflicting operation denial, deletion flag invalidation, expired worker-lease recovery, late acknowledgement after quarantine, stale generations and preserved completed operation IDs. Fresh generated-API typechecking initially found9 inference errors missed by stale local bindings. Explicit validator/handler contracts fixed them; checks were not disabled. Sealed1313-file canonical-kb-search-jobs-r2-20260911 deployed with fresh typechecks to source37.70s and target31.52s; zero generated drift and source hash parity. Admin/Website types and contracts pass:2123functions/2595DTOs/404 existing unknown boundaries. Media coverage1342writes/27owner tables/no bypasses; diffcheck passes.

Actual queued sync on existing dedicated Meilisearch acceptance articles completed on both disposable backends, with persisted provider task IDs source7/target8. Each article was marked synced only after job completion, and current-site search returned it. Jobs: source q98c1yh9ngtagmh2hv3wwk41y18e7z4t, target q98c50zrep1tj30wn42srswrps8e6gn8. Existing restricted keys/indexes/fixtures preserved. No new provider credentials or articles created. Native Electron remains on Aster House staging Our makers, Save changes disabled and one saved preview; main processes preserved. No backend restart or native job-controls acceptance performed in this turn.

Remaining: actual hard-stop recovery of a known provider task and live management expiry/revocation during indexing; operator job UI/start/resume/reconciliation; automatic publish/index integration and bulk rebuild; safe adoption of restored jobs into a new runtime namespace; stale-result fingerprints/access pagination and old shared-index migration; RAG provider acceptance/atomic generations/chunk cleanup/vector scale; KB pagination/permanent deletion/editor/media-warning work;16 missing renderers and all unfinished original24 audit/handoff/auth/isolation/hosting/domain/packaging/recovery/template/editor requirements. ## KB scheduling and real indexing recovery — 2026-09-11

2026-09-11 — Scheduled publishing defects repaired; actual indexing hard-stop recovery accepted.

Confirmed two scheduled-publishing defects with failing handler/database regressions:1005 unscheduled drafts plus250 archived schedules hid a due article and caused a zero-progress immediate reschedule;101 due articles published only50 and failed to continue. Added a status+scheduledAt index and a bounded due-draft scan (50 rows/512KiB read budget), excluding unscheduled/other-status rows before the batch limit. Each publication removes its indexed schedule coordinate; continuation follows remaining due work and an empty nonterminal page fails instead of looping. Registered small-batch tests verify idempotence, category counts, future preservation and search invalidation.

Also reproduced premature kb.article_published emission when merely scheduling a future article, and acceptance of invalid timestamps. Future requests now emit kb.article_scheduled; publication events occur when content actually publishes. Invalid/nonfinite/noninteger/out-of-range timestamps are rejected before mutation. Four failing regressions across the two red checkpoints pass after repair.74 tests/592assertions/7files pass (includes scheduler-handler database tests with scheduling intercepted to prevent the defective loop from running indefinitely). Backend fresh deploy typechecks, Admin/Website types, contracts and media coverage pass;1342classifiedwrites/27owner tables/no bypasses.

### September 14 — Related Content block: implementation and acceptance

Implemented core/related-content end to end. The trusted current document supplies topics and page hierarchy; authored fields cannot supply a source ID. Related posts/pages share current categories or tags, and pages additionally match siblings. The reader excludes the source, unpublished/future/private/password and membership-denied candidates, validates active image media, projects bounded excerpts, and returns actual canonical post/page URLs. Cursor identity binds environment, source, current topics/hierarchy, selected type and limit. Indexed newest-first scans preserve continuation across empty filtered pages and shared source/read budgets. Classifications exceeding 64 relationships refuse explicitly rather than silently truncating. No semantic ranking or unrelated latest-content fallback is claimed.

## September 15 — Shared download entitlement enforcement

commerceDigital/downloadEntitlement.ts now centralizes delivery authorization: active token, safe count/limit, exact expiry boundary, settled payment, nonterminal order, active purchasing identity, item/order/product/file/variant relationships and actual storage existence. Customer-owned tokens cannot be used anonymously or by another account; unowned guest orders retain random-token delivery until ownership is assigned. Old purchased versions and unlisted products remain downloadable when the entitlement is otherwise valid. Public validateDownloadToken projects only file metadata, product title and allowance/expiry rather than raw records. Public/internal record-download mutations share atomic authorization and allowance recording; failures and missing storage produce no success log or allowance consumption. Client diagnostic strings/history are bounded and are not authority. The action is typed without ts-nocheck, and a registered action test proves authentication is propagated to its internal mutation. Legacy storage-URL transport is explicitly documented as reusable rather than falsely temporary.

Fulfillment now uses the same paid-order predicate: completed/processing status alone no longer grants downloads or licenses for pending, failed, refunded or partially paid orders; terminal cancelled/refunded/failed orders are skipped. Existing line-specific partial refund and license lifecycle policy remains unfinished. Legacy internal token generators also still need consolidation; delivery rejects invalid entitlements even if such a token exists.

## September 15 — Secure large-file download transport

Removed commerceDigital/actions:generateDownloadUrl and made both legacy allowance recorders internal-only. New delivery leases authorize the purchaser, store only the hash of a caller-generated secret, pin site/instance/deployment/owner/file identity, expire within one hour, bound pending unstarted requests to eight, and schedule expiry cleanup. Begin/retry is idempotent for the same request and secret. All new registered delivery functions have explicit argument and return validators. The backend exposes a metadata/chunk HTTP route that rechecks current authorization per request, keeps the storage URL internal, validates exact upstream Range/length/encoding, and atomically consumes one allowance when byte issuance starts. A started lease can resume that allowance; another lease cannot spend an exhausted allowance. Success logs describe started issuance, not completed download.

The Website attachment route sets a path-scoped HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie through same-origin POST (Secure on HTTPS), then streams bounded 4 MiB backend chunks with single-range/If-Range support, exact byte counts, safe attachment names, no-store, cancellation and backpressure. It does not buffer the full file or put the secret/storage URL in the download URL. HEAD reads metadata without spending allowance. The dashboard now starts this flow through a typed hook that retains uncertain initiation request identity, clears permanently denied requests, and guards account/site changes.

Live source acceptance after the final deployment: 30,409,237 bytes streamed through the running Website server and matched SHA-256; a 1,048,585-byte resumed interval matched the source ZIP; HEAD worked; allowance count remained one. No-cookie requests, the removed raw-URL action and the former public allowance recorder were denied. The isolated target database denied the same lease with both commerce and digital plugins enabled, and both target settings were restored and read back. A sequential next-chunk request was denied after disabling source digital commerce, then resumed correctly after restoring it, still with one allowance. These are self-hosted fleet/Website HTTP results using the synthetic operator as purchaser; they do not prove distinct customer login, browser/native download UX, cloud Convex or provider streaming behavior. The original verification attempt stopped on an incorrect assumption that target commerce was enabled; the test now saves/enables/restores both target flags. It resumed the existing idempotent lease and did not recreate its order or file.

Fixture state: one new zero-value manual-invoice synthetic order was marked paid, and one private 30.4 MB ZIP is retained for Download Library acceptance. No payment-provider charge was made. Source digital commerce remains enabled for this fixture; its pre-fixture baseline and all fixture identifiers/capabilities are journaled outside the repository. Do not replay prepare-live writes or claim cleanup complete. The ZIP has no public Media Library record. Cleanup must handle its storage blob and retained order audit trail explicitly.

Checks: 60 digital tests / 229 assertions pass after return-validator additions; 9 Website transport tests / 125 assertions pass. The preceding broader commerce/bundles/digital/returns/LMS enrollment suite passed 518 tests / 1,710 assertions. Backend, Admin and Website types pass. Fresh generated contracts pass their check: 2,164 functions / 2,715 terminal DTOs / 373 existing unknown boundaries. Media writer preflight passes. Strict source4860 and target4870 deployments passed 37.33s and 35.56s from canonical-download-transport-r2-20260915; 1,407 files sealed with zero source drift and no further generated API changes. The first transport snapshot is superseded by r2, which adds runtime return validators. Evidence: output/download-transport-20260915.

## September 15 — Download Library renderer #131

Implemented commerce/download-library with a typed viewer host and responsive document-library styling across Core, Journal, Depot and Aster House. It checks order ownership and file/item relationships before projecting private metadata, then uses shared delivery authorization to decide whether a token may be supplied privately to the production host. No billing address, email, storage ID, license key or complete order/product document is returned. ## September 15 — Global stylesheet hydration repair

Resolved the development hydration error identified during Download Library acceptance. The app imported index.css for side effects from router.tsx and also imported index.css?url into a manually managed root head link. Current Vite7.3.1 injects lastHMRTimestamp into URL imports; server and client module graphs produced different URLs after CSS hot updates. The duplicate ownership caused duplicate stylesheet identities and a HeadContent hydration mismatch. Root now owns a single side-effect CSS import, and Start1.159.3 supplies route assets for server rendering. Removed the redundant router import and manual head link. No suppressHydrationWarning workaround, dependency upgrade or running-server restart was introduced. Installed HeadContent/headContentUtils/Vite asset-plugin code was inspected; the approach also matches TanStack's documented route CSS ownership model.

Regression evidence: the fresh browser check failed before the repair with four stylesheet entries but only two normalized identities. It passes afterward with unique stylesheet identities, no page errors, and global CSS applied with JavaScript disabled (body margin0 and rendered Download Library visible). A disposable CSS comment induced a real HMR update; both the updated stylesheet and exact restored source passed the same test. Published-page Sign in navigation now asserts an empty runtime error list and passes. Exact existing Electron PID39198 reopened the connected library preview with fresh pageerror capture empty and CSS present. Existing historical console counters were not reported as new errors. Native Aster House/Staging/Our makers was restored saved.

Production acceptance used an isolated source copy because a live hosting test on4298 consumes the worktree's existing dist. Captured2,293 input files, retained installed third-party dependencies with direct workspace packages remapped to copied sources, built client and server successfully, verified zero build-input drift, and hashed1,250 generated assets. A temporary preview on4330 served the built app against source4860. Two production browser tests passed: public signed-out page/sign-in navigation, unique hashed CSS links, JavaScript-disabled SSR styles and zero page errors. Build emits existing split-chunk/deprecation/browserslist notices; this is not a whole-app performance or hosting acceptance. Active dist and existing application processes were preserved. The owned temporary preview was stopped after testing.

The compiled production download route also streamed the retained30,409,237-byte private ZIP with matching SHA-256 and one additional allowance consumed on the same synthetic operator purchase. This verifies bundled transport execution, not customer login or Cloudflare/Vercel deployment. Its new lease/request/count baseline is in the private download-transport-20260915 journal; do not replay earlier tests that assume the total allowance count remains one. Existing page/order/blob fixtures remain retained. Source Website customer Clerk publishable key/configuration is still absent; real customer browser download remains unverified.

## September 15 — Search Results current-source prerequisite

Continued the unfinished core/search-results block by repairing its existing website search dependency. New search/publicSource.ts builds a closed source-backed display projection: cached index rows supply only candidate identities/ranking, while titles, excerpts, URLs, bylines, dates and taxonomy come from currently authorized documents. Search and autocomplete share it. Current publication dates and malformed/wrong-table IDs are checked; canonical v2 posts/pages never fall back to legacy authored body text. Products, previously always rejected by the search access helper, now enforce Commerce enablement and the shared published-product membership/variant policy, exclude bundle-owned product URLs, and honor the existing category ancestor visibility policy. ## September 15 — Search Results renderer #132 and live pagination repair

Implemented core/search-results and the content.search resolver. The renderer provides a real GET search form, current public result titles/excerpts/bylines, content-kind labels, independent per-block first/next links, idle/empty/filtered-continuation states, responsive pack styling, focus states and reduced motion. Four-pack BlockDemo uses explicitly fictional results and creates no content. Animation uses transform/opacity; frame-budget/GPU performance under production load remains unproven.

The backend validates closed arguments/results, limits queries to500 characters/16 terms and pages to48 results, and binds continuation to the query, kinds, page size, document and website/environment. Candidate identities come from title/body indexes; display text and authorization come from current sources. Events now have publishing/edit/promotion/rollback index synchronization and a bounded internal cursor backfill; one event in each disposable database was processed. The shared search source reader meters actual documents/bytes through RequestReadLedger and uses current post, commerce, course, event, taxonomy and membership authority. Cached private text/account email is not a display source.

Live testing caught a real transport failure absent from in-memory tests: Convex refuses object keys beginning with $. Reproduced with the actual Convex serialization codec; changed $search to search.query, which cannot collide with canonical block IDs. The consumer request and generated foundation copies agree. Admin event result navigation/icon/group labels now cover the extended enum. URL parsing also preserves numeric search terms instead of rejecting TanStack-decoded numbers.

The first live traversal returned4 visible results over152 tiny pages because inaccessible candidates and title/body duplicates consumed page slots. Replaced that behavior with fixed24-row candidate batches, at most4 batches and a96-query local ceiling per block, subject to the existing shared query/document/byte limits. A partially consumed batch carries its start cursor, offset and identity digest, never hidden IDs/text. Changed batch identity is rejected; current source visibility is rechecked on continuation. Bounded work can still produce a filtered continuation page rather than scan without limit. A populated hidden-candidate regression fails against the sealed prior reader and passes now. Final identical live query traverses5 pages,4 unique visible results,0 duplicates. No guessed total, capped search history or unbounded skip loop.

## September 15 — UGC Grid approval prerequisite

The existing media model had no tagged public showcase approval or usage-rights evidence. Added media/showcase get/approve/revoke/tags with closed runtime validators and manage_options authorization. ## September 15 — Synced Content occurrence identities and aggregate data planning

2026-09-15 Synced Content occurrence identities and aggregate data planning. Added a shared pure occurrence plan that preserves saved root/source IDs and digests while assigning distinct stable IDs to reused descendants. Identity is framed by the authored path plus source identity chain; editing/restoring a revision keeps placement identity, while selecting another source changes it. The plan retains wrapper layout/anchors, exact revision ownership and unavailable wrappers, exposes an internal ID index, and derives a separate canonical resolver/navigation tree without storing expansion into authored content. Its dependency digest changes with the resolved revisions. Root ID collisions are refused.

One aggregate data plan applies normal plugin/capability/block policy, cursor binding, unique-job and reserved-output limits to all expanded content; wrappers retain their own runtime policy checks. Repeated data may share reads but still pays for each output binding. Pagination and poll arguments use occurrence IDs, preventing repeated source blocks from sharing interaction identities. Added a service-only published-graph adapter using the same RequestReadLedger for installation, source-head and immutable-revision reads. No anonymous source endpoint or public page activation was added. The pure occurrence/graph modules are generated identically into backend and Website portable closures.

45 relevant foundation/backend tests with311 assertions pass: stable/replaced identities, exact source chains, collisions, pinned/latest/withdrawn behavior, current policy, independent pagination/poll bindings, shared output/node/read budgets, source/post picker regressions and exact foreign installation denial. Backend exact types, Admin/Website types and both generated foundation closure checks pass. No live source/page data was mutated and no native process was restarted.

Repeated polls keep separate ballots; repeated RSVPs intentionally retain one reservation per event/visitor.

## September 15 — Synced Content closed display contract and shared installation

2026-09-15 Synced Content display contract and host data installation. Added a closed synced-display-v1 sidecar carrying bounded exact pinned/latest selections and published source display revisions. Reusable bodies are redacted for both public and editor-preview transport: recipient addresses and lock metadata are removed, and original private-content digests are not exposed. Display digests are computed from the redacted body. Document codecs require sidecars for reusable references, rebuild stable occurrence IDs with the same graph walker used by server publication, enforce current plugin/capability/block policy, and bind dynamic data plus exact display-media references to the expanded tree. Missing, duplicate, unreferenced, mismatched, foreign-installation, cyclic or oversized sidecars are refused.

Refactored the graph into one generator-backed traversal with asynchronous server and synchronous decoder drivers; cache, cycle, revision, node/depth/byte and anchor rules remain shared. Preview-channel digests and public display component keys now include reusable source bindings, so republishing a source invalidates the display even when the containing page revision/digest is unchanged. The shared SSR data installation hook uses the expanded tree rather than the root authoring tree. Registered transport validators derive the new optional shape from the authoritative schema; no new public endpoint was introduced.
